#2061. Ma Kolo Muani chi l'ha voluto alla Juventus? Spalletti fa il Conte per evitare che finisca come all'Inter: uno scambio gli toglierebbe ogni alibi

La Juventus in estate ha speso 41 milioni di euro più dodici di bonus per acquistare a titolo definitivo Randal Kolo Muani. Ha definito l'operazione dopo una telenovela durata oltre un anno, dopo aver già testato il calciatore a Torino nella stagione 2024/25. Non solo: sempre la scorsa estate in una campagna trasferimenti da oltre 120 milioni di acquisti ne ha investiti 16 per acquistare Jeff Ekhator del Genoa e circa tre per il prestito di Nick Woltemade. La società bianconera ha definito queste tre operazioni con l'intenzione di mettere a disposizione di Luciano Spalletti un nuovo centravanti, l'ha fatto perché Jonathan David - altro numero 9 - non era ritenuto adatto alla Juventus del manager di Certaldo. E non lo era nemmeno Lois Openda. L'unico centravanti 'giusto' per Spalletti era Dusan Vlahovic, calciatore volato a Istanbul perché alla Juve voleva guadagnare più di tutti gli altri nonostante un rendimento non all'altezza degli 80 milioni spesi. Che al Besiktas guadagna 8 milioni più bonus dopo aver incassato un bonus alla firma da dieci milioni di euro.

E gli altri? E gli ultimi tre arrivati? Nessuno è adatto: "Abbiamo bisogno di un centravanti perché non l'abbiamo comprato. Kolo Muani lo rimetterò lì perché serve qualcuno che giochi in quella posizione: nel calcio d'oggi serve una punta fisica che sappia giocare spalle alla porta. Poi è inutile parlare di Spalletti via. C'è bisogno di gente fisica, che riceva la pallata addosso e sappia giocare con le mani addosso del centrale avversario. Ora è così. Si vince i duelli. Ci vuole gente fisica che vinca i duelli ed esca dalle mini prigioni che ci sono in campo", le parole di Spalletti dopo la larga vittoria contro il Venezia.

Ma allora chi ha avallato l'acquisto di Kolo Muani e degli altri centravanti? E' possibile che la Juventus abbia speso metà del suo budget estivo per tre centravanti che non erano graditi al suo allenatore? Evidentemente no, anche perché in estate la società bianconera faceva sapere di definire tutte le operazioni dopo un confronto con l'allenatore. E lo stesso Spalletti - dopo aver fatto cadere dalla torre Damien Comolli - esprimeva soddisfazione per il lavoro portato avanti braccio a braccio col nuovo amministratore delegato Giovanni Carnevali.

E allora cosa c'è dietro? Cosa sta succedendo? La verità è che le parole di Antonio Conte non hanno scosso solo l'ambiente bianconero, ma anche lo stesso manager di Certaldo che ha il timore con questa stagione di preparare il terreno al manager leccese proprio come fece all'Inter. Che sabato pomeriggio ha replicato così alla bussata del manager leccese alla porta bianconera: "Io quando non allenavo studiavo gli altri tecnici con rispetto". Un titolo che non è passato inosservato, una conferenza stampa a cui ne ha fatto seguito un'altra in cui Spalletti - proprio come avrebbe fatto Conte - ha esplicitamente chiesto un acquisto per gennaio. Un altro innesto dopo i 9-10 acquisti estivi.

La Juventus in questa situazione è pronta ad assecondare il suo allenatore, l'alternativa sarebbe quella di cambiare guida tecnica e non è questa oggi la strada che vuole seguire la società. Non facile però nella prossima finestra di calciomercato trovare un centravanti all'altezza della Juventus. Le alternative al momento sono principalmente due: o investire una quarantina di milioni per quel Varela che sta facendo sfracelli a Monza ma ha solo poche settimane in Serie A. Oppure - e questa è l'ipotesi più probabile - portare avanti la trattativa con l'Atletico Madrid per cedere Jonathan David a titolo definitivo ai colchoneros e far fare il viaggio opposto a quell'Alexander Sorloth che dall'alto dei suoi 196 centimetri il centravanti di lotta e di governo lo sa fare benissimo.

#2060. Sinner shock, operazione al ginocchio: rientra in primavera

Il tennista sarà operato a Turku il 24 novembre; le prime stime indicano un recupero di circa tre-quattro mesi.

Jannik Sinner ha deciso di sottoporsi a un intervento al ginocchio destro. L’operazione è programmata per martedì 24 novembre a Turku, in Finlandia, e sarà eseguita dal professor Lasse Lempainen. La scelta arriva dopo diversi consulti medici e oltre quattro mesi di terapie conservative che non hanno dato i risultati sperati.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Le prime stime indicano un recupero di circa tre-quattro mesi. Il rientro potrebbe avvenire in primavera, ma i tempi dipenderanno dall’esito dell’intervento e dalla successiva riabilitazione.

Il 12 luglio Sinner aveva conquistato il suo secondo Wimbledon consecutivo battendo Alexander Zverev in finale. Quella partita è stata anche la sua ultima apparizione ufficiale della stagione. Nelle settimane successive il tennista italiano ha cominciato ad avvertire dolore al ginocchio destro.

Il problema è stato identificato come una sindrome della bandelletta ileotibiale, che provoca dolore nella parte esterna dell’articolazione. Per un atleta sottoposto a sollecitazioni continue può diventare particolarmente fastidiosa.

All’inizio Sinner e il suo gruppo di lavoro avevano scelto di evitare la chirurgia. Il percorso prevedeva riposo, fisioterapia e trattamenti specifici, con l’obiettivo di recuperare gradualmente la piena funzionalità del ginocchio senza correre rischi inutili o compromettere ulteriormente la carriera del tennista.

Nonostante i tentativi e i consulti con diversi specialisti, il dolore non è scomparso definitivamente. Il numero due del mondo ha quindi preso in considerazione l’intervento. Decisivo è stato l’ultimo confronto con Lempainen, chirurgo ortopedico finlandese specializzato nel trattamento delle lesioni muscolari e tendinee degli sportivi professionisti.

Tra gli atleti già seguiti dal medico figurano anche i calciatori Ousmane Dembélé e Leonardo Spinazzola. Dopo la valutazione della situazione clinica di Sinner, la scelta è ricaduta sull’operazione a Turku.

#2059. ‘It was a matter of time’: Norwich City vs QPR predictions

Norwich City come into their midweek clash with QPR as one of the Championship’s form sides. The Canaries have now won five league matches on the trot, leaving their poor start to the season in the past and surging up the table.

Philippe Clement’s men rose to 11th in the league with a 2-1 victory over a dangerous Cardiff City side on Saturday. Paris Maghoma scored his fifth goal in five consecutive games, and Mathias Kvistgaarden continued his solid sophomore season with his fifth of the campaign as well.

QPR make the trip to Carrow Road in a confident mood. They were victorious themselves on Saturday, with returning Danish midfielder Nicolas Madsen scoring and starring in a 1-0 away win over 10-man Millwall.

The Hoops have strengthened their grip on a Championship play-off spot as a result, moving into 7th. It was their first away win in five league matches, so Julien Stephan will hope his side can find some consistent form on the road heading into the hectic winter schedule.

“Norwich City’s winning run has seen them surge back into the play-off conversation. It did feel like it was a matter of time before they found form, but I certainly didn’t anticipate them reeling off five consecutive Championship wins.

“Philippe Clement has had to deal with some injury problems but he’s got the squad to get results. Paris Maghoma is a real maverick in attacking midfield, and Mathias Kvistgaarden coming good in his second season has massively softened the blow of losing Mohamed Toure.

“QPR are going to be a really tough opponent for the Canaries though. The Hoops are prone to big losses on the road – as shown by 3-0 and 4-1 defeats to Birmingham City and Southampton. They’re a team brimming with talent from back to front, and I think they can be a great match for the hosts here.

“But, given the visitors’ away form and Norwich City’s recent success, I am leaning towards a home win. This should be a good game, and don’t write off QPR.”

“Norwich City are on a roll and will fancy their chances of a sixth win on the bounce. NCFC’s players will be full of confidence and they look like genuine promotion contenders.

“The Canaries took a while to get going earlier this season but they appear to have fully clicked now under Philippe Clement. They have won their last two matches at Carrow Road on the spin and are starting to make their home ground a fortress.

“QPR are no pushovers though and will be tough opponents for the hosts here. They are in the top eight for a reason and Julien Stephan has done a brilliant job with the London outfit.

“The Hoops have lost only one of their last five and that was to Southampton. I think they will be good for a useful point in East Anglia on Tuesday night.”

#2058. Avellino, Aiello sul mercato: "Monitoriamo le opportunità, siamo pronti a farci trovare preparati"

Mario Aiello, direttore sportivo dell'Avellino, è stato intervistato da Radio Kiss Kiss.

“La Serie B è un campionato difficilissimo dove dalla prima all'ultima sono tutti attrezzati. Ci sono risultati sorprendenti. Noi per fortuna siamo riusciti ad avere un certo andamento equilibrato, abbiamo fatto qualche risultato importante e stiamo cercando di crescere partita dopo partita per consolidare questa categoria”.

“I tifosi hanno sempre diritto e dovere di poter sognare perché nel calcio non si può mai sapere. Noi che chiaramente ci operiamo all'interno abbiamo invece il dovere di essere sempre equilibrati e di cercare di fare i passi uno alla volta cercando di consolidare questo club, sia dal punto di vista tecnico, sia a livello di tutte quelle che sono le strutture che possono far crescere il nostro valore. Nel calcio tutto può succedere, sicuramente dobbiamo andare avanti mantenendo sempre un profilo basso ma cercando di pensare in grande".

"Lui ha sempre avuto talento, qualità e forza. Chiaramente come tutti i giovani hanno avuto bisogno di tempo per maturare sotto tanti aspetti sotto il profilo tattico e caratteriale. Ora lui è più andato avanti, è più acquisito consapevolezza dei propri mezzi e adesso probabilmente è uno dei calciatori che in Serie B fa più la differenza e secondo me ha i numeri, la forza, il motore e la qualità per poter essere protagonista anche in Serie A".

“La società non si tira indietro, però riteniamo di avere una squadra abbastanza completa e quindi se in questa fase stiamo semplicemente monitorando quello che può essere il meglio in termini prospettici anche per il nostro club. Se qualcuno dovesse chiedere di andare via o per scarso utilizzo o per altri motivi noi sicuramente ci possiamo far trovare pronti e poi vediamo un attimino qual è l'eventuale opportunità da cogliere”. #2055. ‘Another defeat’: Wolves vs Derby County predictions

Wolves come into Tuesday night’s clash with Derby County after extending their Championship unbeaten run to seven games with a 2-1 win over Charlton Athletic. Cesar Peixoto’s side started the second half with a flurry as Adam Armstrong and Rafiki Said netted in the 51st and 54th minutes, both set up by Mateus Mane.

The victory moves Wanderers into 3rd in the table, behind only Stoke City and Middlesbrough. They have lost just once in their eight home league games this term too, winning all of the last three.

As for Derby County, they make the trip to Molineux off the back of a 2-0 loss away at Southampton. It marked a tough fixture for DCFC as they looked to build on a 3-1 win over Bolton Wanderers before the international break.

It has been a tough season for the Rams, who remain 23rd in the table with just 10 points from 16 games. John Eustace will hope form can start to turn with the return of some key players though, with Patrick Agyemang, Matt Clarke and Eiran Cashin all closing in on comebacks.

“Wolves are in great form and in a good place in the Championship table. Cesar Peixoto had to weather a bit of a storm in the early stages of his tenure but their seven-game unbeaten streak has seen them collect 17 points while conceding only five goals.

“Mateus Mane was excellent against Charlton Athletic and it will be hoped he can really kick on after a fairly quiet campaign to date. Rafiki Said looks like an exciting player, and Adam Armstrong can be relied on to score goals at Championship level.

“I think Old Gold will have too much for the Derby County defence. DCFC would have hoped to build on that Bolton Wanderers win, but away games at Southampton and Wolves mean they don’t have a great chance of building momentum and clambering out of the bottom three.

“I’ll be going for a home win here, and in fairly routine fashion.”

“Wolves look like serious top two contenders and they will see their upcoming clash as another opportunity to pick up three points. WWFC have won their last three home games without conceding a goal.

“Cesar Peixoto’s men haven’t lost since their defeat to Bolton Wanderers in mid-October. They are well organised and have dangerous threats at the top end of the pitch.

“Derby County continue to struggle for regular points and they are at serious risk of slipping into League One this season. DCFC have won only two of their 16 matches in the league this term which is a massive worry.

“The Rams’ injury issues have been well documented and pressure continues to grow on John Eustace. I think they will slump to another defeat on their travels here.”

#2054. Chelsea’s feisty friendly ends in penalty shoot-out confusion

Chelsea’s Far East tour ended in farce and confusion with players waiting around for a penalty shoot-out that never happened.

The chaos came at the end of a hard-earned, bad-tempered draw against Malaysian champions Johor Darul Ta’zim, with an own-goal in the 89th minute sparring the west London side’s blushes.

That goal also was supposed to set up penalties. But after minutes of waiting around – and Kevin Campello, Chelsea’s head of first-team operations, saying “it’s written in the contract” – the players walked off the pitch.

That confusion ended a feisty affair in which tensions threatened to boil over and that, at certain points, paid little attention to the “friendly” aspect of the clash with seven yellow cards shown. At one point the visitors’ new set-piece coach Austin MacPhee exchanged angry words with some of Johor’s coaching staff. The CFC TV commentary team suggested that the spot-kicks were abandoned because of the match’s heated atmosphere.

Austin MacPhee was ready to throw hands😂 pic.twitter.com/CFDgTOwmxE

Having played just 24 hours earlier in Jakarta – a 3-0 win over AC Milan – it was no surprise to see Xabi Alonso name a fresh XI, a mixture of players who may well be offloaded before the end of the transfer window, those needing to prove themselves, and youngsters given much-needed game time.

And considering Johor are one game away from setting a new world record of 109 matches unbeaten in the top flight – a run that would surpass the current mark set by Ivorian side Asec Mimosas in the early 1990s – it was also no shock to see the Malaysian team offer a stern, pre-season test for the west Londoners.

After a good start Chelsea were second best in a first half in which Johor should have been at least two goals to the good. The Malaysian champions controlled the first 45 minutes and were 1-0 up after 14 minutes – a low cross from the left side-footed home by Arif Aiman.

The hosts gave the young, inexperienced Chelsea backline a torrid time, especially on the flanks and Óscar Arribas was guilty of not capitalising on a great chance to double Johor’s lead, his shot after cutting in from the right going wide.

While they looked vulnerable at the back, Chelsea also struggled to keep possession in attack. But they got an equaliser they scarcely deserved after Liam Delap was manhandled to the ground in the box by Kevin Medina. The striker, who may well leave Stamford Bridge before the transfer window slams shut, fired home the spot-kick allowing the visitors to go into the break on level terms.

For the most part, the second half followed a similar pattern to the first – Jofor having the better chances, while tensions on the pitch continued to run high.

It was, however, Chelsea who took the lead after the break, Delap once again scoring from the spot, this time after Jamie Gittens was brought down in the area.

Johor, though, kept on creating, thanks in part to a fairly porous visitors’ backline, and levelled through a deflected Arribas free kick, before going 3-2 up thanks thanks to a well-taken Bérgson strike.

Had it stayed that way Chelsea could have had few complaints. But as the clock approached 90 minutes, the visitors had one last attack. Mykhailo Mudryk, just on as a substitute, delivered a cross from the left that Antonio Glauder could only steer into his own net. That was supposed to set up the penalty shoot-out that did not take place and the chance for one of the sides to claim a hollow victory.

Considering a raft of changes and that fact this was far from the first XI, it is hard to know what to make of this. But among the positive things Alonso could take from the game was the impressive form of Nicolas Jackson and good centre-forward play of Delap. The negative was, however, a poor defensive display.

Chelsea return to Stamford Bridge on Saturday for their last pre-season friendly, against Real Sociedad, before the Premier League season starts away at local rivals Fulham on August 24.

Johor Darul Ta’zim: Syihan Hazmi; Arribas, Israfilov (Carlos Akapo 82), Medina, Antonio Glauder, Silva (La’Vere Corbin-Ong 89); Arif Aiman, Petrovic, Méndez (Nené 78), Marcos Guilherme (Bérgson); Zahedi (Stoichkov 62). Substitutes: Abad, Davies, Hidalgo, Nahuel Leiva, Lowry, Mohamadou Sumareh, Stuart Wilkin.Booked: Israfilov, Antonio Glauder, Silva.Goals: Arif Aiman (14), Arribas (65), Bérgson (86).Chelsea: Sharman-Lowe (Slolina 46); Subuloye, Adarabioyo, Anselmino (Watson 29); Geovany Quenda, Dario Essugo, Nicoll-Jazuli, Gittens (Mudryk 87); Estêvão, Delap, Jacskon (Satpaev 86). Substitutes: Curd, Diakite, Kavuma-McQueen, Sarr, Walsh.Booked: Estêvão, Delap, Jackson, Geovany Quenda, Watson.Goals: Delap (42 pen, 62 pen), Antonio Glauder (89 og).

There will NOT be a penalty shootout. No one is quite sure why.

Anyway, that was an entertaining draw, plenty of good things for Chelsea (the play of Delap and Jackson) as well as worrying signs (open and vulnerable at the back).

The commentators on CFC TV are now not so sure!

That was an entertaining pre-season friendly. There was a bit of niggle and that added to the action.

There will be penalties, apparently, if it stays the same score...

Will there be a late winner? There will be four minutes of added time.

Mudryk just on picks up the ball on the left and, with four Chelsea players in the box, crosses into the danger area. It’s a good ball and asks too many questions of the hosts as Glauder diverts the ball into his own net.

It’s what the host have, on the balance of play, deserved, but it’s a gift from Chelsea. Essugo loses the ball in the final third and Bergson, on as sub pick it put driving at the box before hitting a low shot from outside the area, the ball nestling in the left corner of the net.

Chelsea have been better this half than the first 45, they’ve been able to control the pace of the game well since the break.

The pace has slowed down since the break, Chelsea seeing more of the ball. They work it right before Watson loses possession on the edge of the area.

The referee clearly forgot about the hydration break and the two teams are having a belated one now. Delightful...

I reckon there’s another goal left in this match. It’s been entertaining fare and the hosts have given as good as they’ve got, if not more.

This has been a lively match, better than most run-of-the-mill pre-season friendlies. Chelsea seeing a fair bit of possession at the moment, can they fashion a chance?

Yes they can. But it’s a longish shot from Gittens that is easily saved by Hamzi in the JDT goal.

More niggle in this match that hasn’t really had many tough challenges. After a few handbags the referee takes control and the argy bargy peters out.

It’s the least the hosts deserve. They have a free-kick 25 yards out, Arribas takes it with his right it’s at the wall but in turning away from the ball Delap deflects it into the far-right corner giving Slonina no chance.

Fluky goal but the hosts have deserved their bit of luck.

Delap again with the goal, this time he goes low, but still to the keeper’s right.

Penalty for Chelsea. Gittens cuts back in the box onto his right and is clipped by the defender. Clear-cut penalty.

Half-chance for Delap on the edge of the area. He playes a one-two with Jackson before opening up his body and letting fly, the shot going well wide.

Another good chance for the hosts and it’s not shock to write that it’s come from the industry of Arribas on the right. He fires in a low cross that evades everyone in the box.

Estevao goes down in the box, it looks as though it could well be a penalty but the referee waves play on. The Brazilian is the shown a yellow card for possibly lashing out having not got the decision he wanted.

The hosts have asserted themselves well this half, in much the same way as they did in the first 45 minutes.

Another good chance for the hosts and again it’s all too easy as Zahedi is one-on-one with the keeper. The striker, however, dithers a bit too long and Slonina makes a fine save. JDT have had plenty of chances to add to their sole goal...

Chelsea have had a ragged start to this half.

Good work from JDT who keep the ball well in midfield before a throughball is played to the on-rushing Zahedi but Slonina comes out well.

They’re back under way in Johor, and there’s an early chance for Jackson who gets the ball in the box, but the ball gets stuck underneath him and he cannot get in a good attempt in on goal.

And that is the last action of an entertaining half. Chelsea started well before the hosts opened the scoring. JDT then took control of the half and should, if truth be told, have extended their lead. But Delap did well to earn the penalty that he subsequently scored and should have made it 2-1 with the last kick of the 45.

Worrying signs at the back, however, for Alonso. Will Chelsea go to four at the back after the break?

The answer to that question (see last post) was so very nearly yes...

Jackson plays in a peach of a through ball with the outside of his right foot, Delap is one-on-one with the keeper and looks for all the world as though he has to score. BUT he scuffs his shot wide.

There will be three minutes of added time. Can Chelsea head into the break with an unexpected lead?

Free-kick for Chelsea on the JDT byline. The ball is put deep into the box, it’s headed back by Tosin before the ball is blazed over the bar.

A minute later Tosin then let’s fly form 25 yards, the ball goes well over the bar.

The hosts should have been more than 1-0 to the good and now they’re drawing. Goals change matches...

Having won the penalty Delap fires the spotkick home, sending the keeper the the wrong way.

Penalty to Chelsea and it’s all thanks to good centre-forward play by Delap. He gets the ball in the box and Medina manhands him to the ground. The referee doesn’t hesitate before pointing to the spot.

Chelsea have struggled to create anything since the opening 15 minutes. They have no control at the front and none at the back currently.

As I type that JDT have another good chance to turn the 1-0 lead into a 2-0 lead. Arribas, whose been a menace all game, dances toward the Chelsea box before delaying the pass too long.

JDT are finding a lot of joy on the flanks, there are three at the back for Chelsea and on the outside are two youngsters. The hosts are working the space well.

Yet again Chelsea are made to look decidedly average. A simple ball over the top on the right opens up the visitors’ defence and Arribas is in on goal, he cuts in and lets fly with his left peg, the shot going wide.

That should have been 2-0 to the Malaysian powerhouses.

Worrying signs for Chelsea who have struggled to keep possession up top and looked vulnerable at the back.

Yet more good work from JDT who are being given space by Chelsea. Marcos Guilherme slaloms into the west Londoners’ box and Tosin again doesn’t look the most assured but the JDT player cuts the ball back and the visitors can clear the danger.

Another good attack from the hosts who get in a shot in on goal, it’s deflected and loops into the grateful arms of Sharman-Lowe in the Chelsea goal.

There’s a break in play with Anselmino down and suffering what looks like a hamstring injury. He is eventually replaced by Reggie Watson.

Meanwhile, the home fans are in fine voice and waving several HUGE flags.

Yep, everyone’s least favourite part of the World Cup in the U, S of A is back. To be fair it’s humid in Johor so taking on fluids during the first 45 minutes is no bad idea.

There’s a bit of argy-bargy not really what you should get in a ‘friendly’. There hasn’t been a bad chellenge in the match so far so not sure where that came from...

Yet more good work from the hosts, this time it’s down the right, Arribas getting the better of Anselmino before shooting at the near post - the attempt hitting the side netting.

After a lively start Chelsea have been second best.

Poor defending from the visitors there but the chance was well snapped up by JDT.

Lovely break from the hosts. They break down the left at pace, before the ball is threaded through to the back post and Arif Aiman sidefoots into the back of the net with aplomb.

So very nearly an opener for Chelsea. They break at pace thanks to Estevao who drives down the middle before playing in Jackson. The striker cuts in from the left and shoots, the attempt is saved well by Hamzi and Estevao’s follow up is wide. Is a goal coming?

Free-kick for Chelsea some 35 yards out. Too far fro a shot on goal? Of course it is. The ball is floated into the box and the visitors earn a corner.

What can they create from this set piece? ‘Not much’ is the initial answer as the ball is cleared by JDT but Chelsea win possession back and the ball is fed to Estevao on the left, the Brazilian goes for go with his trusty left peg but the bal goes high and wide.

JDT attack down the left, it’s a lovely ball in that forces a fine header to clear the danger from Adarabioyo.

No sooner had I sent that last post than Chelsea break through the middle. The ball is fed through to Delap who gets a left-footed shot in on goal forcing a good save from the JDT keeper Hamzi.

Quiet, low-key start to the match. No real shock there, it is a pre-season friendly.

JDT give the ball away in their half and Chelsea have a half-decent platform to launch an attack from. They cannot fashion a chance and also lose possession.

They’re under way in southern Malaysia, Chelsea are in their vibrant blue and the hosts are wearing a fetching pink number. There was a late change for the visitors, Mamadou Sarr has been replaced by Olutayo Subuloye.

CHELSEA XI TO FACE JOHOR: Sharman-Lowe; Sarr, Tosin, Anselmino; Quenda, Nicoll-Jazuli, Essugo (c), Gittens; Estevao; Jackson, Delap; subs: Slonina, Curd, Subuloye, Diakite, Watson, Mudryk, Walsh, Kavuma-McQueen, Satpayev

...Luis Garcia. The Spaniard has history with Chelsea, remember the ‘ghost goal’?

Arriving for duty! 🫡 pic.twitter.com/M2XEkLC18n

Your Chelsea side today! 🔵 pic.twitter.com/0OvvGVmgpc

A mixture of players who may well be offloaded before the end of the transfer window, those needing to prove themselves, and youngsters you may not have heard of.

Johor Darul Ta’Zim – more commonly known as JDT – are the Man Utd of Malaysia (at least that’s according to a Malaysian friend of mine). In 2013, they were going nowhere and were the dictionary definition of a stagnant club, low on confidence and trophies. That was until the Crown Prince bought the club and, over the next 13 years, transformed it into the powerhouse it is today.

As if to underline JDT’s dominance over Malaysian football they are one game away from the world record unbeaten run in a top flight. The new season of the Malaysian Super League gets under way later this month and should they avoid defeat in their opening match they will extend their unbeaten run - which began in April 2021 - to 109 games and surpass the world record set by Ivorian side Asec Mimosas in the early 1990s.

Chelsea’s pre-season tour of South East Asia ends today with a match against Johor Darul Ta’zim in Malaysia. The clash comes barely 24 hours after the west Londoners beat AC Milan 3-0 in Jakarta, a much-needed win coming on the back of defeats against Juventus and Tottenham.

A fine volley from Moises Caicedo and a double from João Pedro either side of half-time led Xabi Alonso’s side to victory in the Indonesian capital.

The game was fairly even in the first half and looked set to head into the break level at 0-0 but Pedro’s header from Caicedo’s corner put Chelsea in front.

Alonso’s side then doubled their advantage seconds into the second half as Pedro scored his second, assisted by Pedro Neto.

Just minutes later, Chelsea had their third courtesy of a well-worked corner routine. Romeo Lavia’s delivery was met by Caicedo on the volley, with the Ecuadorian’s finish with the outside of the boot finding the bottom corner.

Today, the west London side face Malaysian powerhouses Johor at the Sultan Ibrahim Stadium, more on them later, but considering the tight turnaround from yesterday’s match today’s clash will provide a stern test.

“The idea, with the game against Milan and then one in Johor, was to arrive at this weekend with players not being ready for 90 minutes until now,” Alonso explained after the win in Jakarta.

“We wanted to have a logical progression in their loads; so we had our plan and we’ve stuck to that.

“We have another idea [against Johor]; that was decided yesterday [Friday]. The idea is that many young players were coming to have chances to play tomorrow. If not, we’d have been short, as playing two games in two days is quite intense. So we will need them.”

#2053. Mercato Napoli, c’è anche il Como di Fabregas su Zirkzee dello United: i dettagli di SKY

Complice l’ infortunio di Kean che lo sta tenendo fuori dal campo a lungo, il Como comincia le valutazioni in vista della finestra invernale di calcio mercato, in particolare proprio sul reparto di attacco. Tra le varie ipotesi, può esserci anche il nome di Joshua Zirkzee , che dopo aver salutato il Bologna ed essere approdato al Manchester United è stato più volte accostato a un ritorno in Serie A.

Ma non solo, perché anche il Napoli è sull’olandese, profilo che piace in caso di addio di Lorenzo Lucca a gennaio. Entrambe le squadre stanno sondando la possibilità e quindi il suo nome potrebbe tornare a essere un tormentone.

LA SITUAZIONE – Il classe 2001 è una possibilità che quindi le due società stanno esplorando, senza che ci sia già una reale trattativa. Una variabile da considerare, poi, è Mauricio Pochettino , appena arrivato sulla panchina del Manchester United. Infatti, sarà da capire se l’allenatore partirà l’attaccante e, in quel caso, a quali condizioni.

#2052. Occhio Inzaghi, il River tenta Bounou: il marocchino sogna di giocare con i Millonarios

Il River Plate ha già iniziato a programmare le mosse per la stagione 2027. La dirigenza dei Millonarios sta lavorando soprattutto in vista di una possibile partenza di Santiago Beltran, finito nel mirino di diversi club europei dopo una stagione da protagonista.

Il giovane portiere argentino ha attirato l’attenzione grazie alle sue prestazioni, tanto da ricevere la convocazione di Lionel Scaloni per gli ultimi impegni della Selección e debuttare nella vittoria contro l’Honduras. Sul classe 2006 si sarebbero posati gli occhi di Aston Villa e Atletico Madrid, ma non solo. Il River non vorrebbe ostacolare una sua eventuale cessione, ma intende tutelarsi e sta trattando il rinnovo del contratto fino al 2030, con adeguamento dell’ingaggio e soprattutto un aumento della clausola rescissoria. Il club sarebbe pronto ad ascoltare offerte soltanto da 15 milioni di dollari in su.

Per questo motivo la società argentina ha iniziato a muoversi per un possibile erede. Il principale candidato è Yassine Bounou, portiere marocchino dell’Al Hilal, per il quale alcuni emissari del River avrebbero già avviato contatti in Arabia Saudita. Il classe 1991 ha più volte espresso il suo legame con il club argentino e il desiderio di difenderne un giorno la porta: "È qualcosa che ho in sospeso, sinceramente. È uno dei sogni che ho e mi piacerebbe che un giorno si realizzasse. Non voglio farlo solo per indossare lo stemma: voglio arrivare e rendere al massimo". Il suo contratto con l’Al Hilal scade nel 2028.

#2051. Birmingham City summer signing who has played just two minutes of Championship football will start vs Burnley: Opinion

Birmingham City saw their impressive form halted on Saturday as they lost 1-0 away at fellow high-flyers Stoke City. It was a frustrating day for BCFC, but recent evidence suggests they’re well placed to return to winning ways when they face Burnley on Wednesday night.

The Blues are 4th in the Championship table despite Saturday’s loss, but the result wasn’t the only dampener on a tricky day at the office. Club captain and standout defender Christoph Klarer was booked in the first half, and as it was his fifth yellow card of the campaign, he now faces a one-match suspension.

Birmingham City aren’t blessed with strength in numbers at centrre-back, but in rarely-seen summer signing Dael Fry, Chris Davies has a fine player to call upon to cover for the absence of star man Klarer.

Fry has been a Championship mainstay for almost his entire career. He came through the academy of hometown club Middlesbrough and barring a stint on loan at Rotherham United, he had spent his full playing career at the Riverside Stadium.

He marked a strong free transfer signing for Birmingham City in the summer. However, after 16 games of the Championship campaign, Fry has made just two substitute appearances, totalling only two minutes. On both occasions, he came on off the bench to help see out 2-1 victories – first over Wrexham, and then Derby County.

Fry has been unused on the BCFC bench on 12 occasions, but as the only other centre-back alongside regular starters Klarer and Phil Neumann, the 29-year-old is bound to come into the team at Burnley.

Losing Klarer is far from ideal, but Fry is a fine replacement. Wednesday night will bring his 275th Championship appearance, and he offers a strong aerial presence at the back while thriving in duels and one-on-one defending. He can be trusted in possession as well, so should slot into Davies’ defence with ease.

Upon swapping Middlesbrough for Birmingham City, Fry will have hoped for more minutes than he has found. Klarer and Neumann have been an unbreakable partnership though, forcing the summer signing to watch on from the bench more often than not.

Given his status as a Championship stalwart, that will have frustrated the former Boro ace. But, Fry’s influence will have been felt through his leadership and professionalism, offering a strong presence in the dressing room and on the training ground.

This clash with Burnley offers a welcome chance for Fry to show BCFC fans that he is more than ready to deliver when called upon, despite the fact he is a clear third-choice central defender at St Andrew’s.

#2050. Napoli, De Bruyne andrà al PSV o a gennaio o a giugno: ora c’è distanza col Napoli (SKY)

Gianluca Di Marzio, giornalista ed esperto di mercato, ha parlato del Napoli e di De Bruyne a Sky Sport: “Si è parlato in questi giorni dell’offerta del PSV e la confermiamo: ha messo sul piatto 2 milioni di euro. Ha un accordo di massima con il giocatore, ha la disponibilità di De Bruyne a riavvicinarsi a casa. Se non sarà a gennaio, sarà a giugno. Da capire a che condizioni il Napoli lo lascerà partire e se lo lascerà partire. E qual è veramente la volontà di De Bruyne, se aspetterà giugno oppure no. Due milioni è la cifra che il PSV ha messo sul tavolo, ma che il Napoli non accetta assolutamente: il club azzurro chiederà una cifra molto più alta”.
